Nonlinear stability of 2-D Couette flow for the compressible Navier-Stokes equations at high Reynolds number
Minling Li, Chao Wang, Zhifei Zhang

Abstract
In this paper, we investigate the nonlinear stability of the Couette flow for the two-dimensional compressible Navier–Stokes equations at high Reynolds numbers( R ​ e Re ) regime. It was proved that if the initial data ( ρ i ​ n , 𝒖 i ​ n ) (\rho_{in},\bm{u}_{in}) satisfies ‖ ( ρ i ​ n , 𝒖 i ​ n ) − ( 1 , y , 0 ) ‖ H 4 ​ ( 𝕋 × ℝ ) ≤ ϵ ​ R ​ e − 1 \|(\rho_{in},\bm{u}_{in})-(1,y,0)\|_{H^{4}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})}\leq\epsilon Re^{-1} for some small ϵ \epsilon independent of R ​ e Re , then the corresponding solution exists globally and remains close to the Couette flow for all time. Formal asymptotics indicate that this stability threshold is sharp within the class of Sobolev perturbations. The proof relies on the Fourier-multiplier method and exploits three essential ingredients: (i) the introduction of “good unknowns” that decouple the perturbation system; (ii) the construction of a carefully designed Fourier multiplier that simultaneously captures the enhanced dissipation and inviscid-damping effects while taming the lift-up mechanism; and (iii) the design of distinct energy functionals for the incompressible and compressible modes.



1
Introduction

In this paper, we consider the two-dimensional isentropic compressible Navier-Stokes equations in a domain
{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\rho+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}(\rho\bm{u})=0,\\
\partial_{t}(\rho\bm{u})+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}(\rho\bm{u}\otimes\bm{u})+\frac{1}{M}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mathcal{P}(\rho)=\mu\Delta_{\bm{x}}\bm{u}+(\mu+\mu^{\prime})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{u},\end{array}\right.

where
\rho(t,\bm{x})
,
\bm{u}(t,\bm{x})=(u^{1},u^{2})^{T}(t,\bm{x})
and
\mathcal{P}(\rho)
represent the density, the velocity and the pressure, respectively. The pressure
\mathcal{P}(\rho)
here is assumed to be a smooth function in
a neighborhood of
1
with
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(1)>0
.
Here,
M>0
is the Mach number,
\mu
and
\mu^{\prime}
are the shear viscosity and the bulk viscosity coefficients, respectively, satisfying the following physical condition

\displaystyle\mu>0,\quad\mu+\mu^{\prime}\geq 0.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(1)=1
and
M=1
in this paper. The initial data of (
1
) is given by

\displaystyle(\rho,\bm{u})|_{t=0}=(\rho_{in},\bm{u}_{in})(\bm{x}).

In this paper, we study the nonlinear stability of the Couette flow given by

\displaystyle(\bar{\rho},\bar{\bm{u}})(t,\bm{x})=(1,(y,0)^{T}).

Thus, we introduce
(n,\bm{v})
to be the perturbation of
(\rho,\bm{u})
around
(\bar{\rho},\bar{\bm{u}})
, i.e.,

\displaystyle n\triangleq\rho-\bar{\rho},\quad\bm{v}\triangleq\bm{u}-\bar{\bm{u}},

(1.3)

which satisfies the following perturbation system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}n+y\partial_{x}n+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}=F_{1},\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}\bm{v}+y\partial_{x}\bm{v}+(v^{2},0)^{T}+\nabla_{\bm{x}}n-\mu\Delta_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}-(\mu+\mu^{\prime})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}=\bm{F}_{2},\\[4.30554pt]
(n,\bm{v})|_{t=0}=(n_{in},\bm{v}_{in})(\bm{x})=(\rho_{in}-\bar{\rho},\bm{u}_{in}-\bar{\bm{u}}),\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle F_{1}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\bm{v}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}n-n\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v},

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{2}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\bm{v}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}-\left(\frac{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(1+n)}{1+n}-1\right)\nabla_{\bm{x}}n-\frac{n}{1+n}\left(\mu\Delta_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}+(\mu+\mu^{\prime})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}\right).

(1.7)

1.1
Background and known results

The stability of plane Couette flow has been investigated since the seminal works of Rayleigh
[
37
]
and Kelvin
[
27
]
. Romanov
[
15
,
39
]
proved that Couette flow is spectrally stable for all Reynolds numbers, a result that appears to contradict both numerical simulations and physical experiments
[
10
,
20
,
40
,
41
,
42
,
46
]
, which indicate that shear flows become unstable and undergo transition to turbulence at sufficiently high Reynolds numbers. This discrepancy is known as the Sommerfeld paradox. To elucidate the mechanisms underlying transition to turbulence, Trefethen et al.
[
42
]
first formulated the transition threshold problem: namely, to quantify the magnitude of perturbations required to trigger instability and to determine their scaling with the Reynolds number. More recently, Bedrossian, Germain, and Masmoudi
[
5
,
6
]
provided a rigorous mathematical framework for this problem, stated as follows.

Given a norm
\|\cdot\|_{X}
, find a
\beta=\beta(X)
so that

\displaystyle\|u_{0}\|_{X}\leq Re^{-\beta}\to\textrm{stability},

\displaystyle\|u_{0}\|_{X}\geq Re^{-\beta}\to\textrm{instability}.

The exponent
\beta
is referred to as the transition threshold.

A substantial body of work in applied mathematics and physics has been devoted to estimating the exponent
\beta
(see, e.g.,
[
2
,
10
,
16
,
28
,
32
,
38
,
43
,
46
]
). Over the past decade, rigorous mathematical results have appeared in rapid succession. For the two-dimensional incompressible case without physical boundaries (i.e.,
{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}
), Bedrossian, Masmoudi, and Vicol
[
8
]
proved that
\beta=0
when the perturbation space
X
is of Gevrey class
2+
. If
X
is taken to be a Sobolev space, subsequent works
[
36
,
44
]
have established the upper
\beta\leq\frac{1}{3}
. In the finite channel
{\mathbb{T}}\times[-1,1]
, Chen, Li, Wei, and the third author
[
13
]
derived
\beta\leq\frac{1}{2}
for the two-dimensional problem under the no-slip boundary condition. Further two-dimensional stability results for Couette flow can be found in
[
9
,
35
,
30
,
7
]
. The picture changes significantly in three dimensions because of a new linear mechanism—the lift-up effect—that induces transient growth of disturbances. Consequently, the transition threshold problem becomes substantially more difficult. For the domain
{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{T}}
, Bedrossian, Germain, and Masmoudi
[
3
,
4
,
5
]
obtained
\beta\leq 1
when
X
is a Gevrey space and
\beta\leq\frac{3}{2}
when
X
is a Sobolev space. Wei and the third author
[
45
]
later improved the Sobolev result to
\beta\leq 1
. Most recently, Chen, Wei, and the third author
[
14
]
treated the channel geometry
{\mathbb{T}}\times[-1,1]\times{\mathbb{T}}
under the no-slip boundary condition and again derived
\beta\leq 1
for Sobolev perturbations.

Although the stability of compressible flows has a long history, the stability of compressible Couette flow remains far less understood than its incompressible counterpart. The compressible system is inherently more complicated, and the associated lift-up effect is markedly stronger. To date, most investigations have been numerical or physical (see, e.g.,
[
18
,
19
,
23
,
24
,
33
,
22
,
11
,
12
,
17
]
). On the mathematical side, Kagei
[
26
]
established asymptotic stability of Couette flow for small Reynolds numbers, while Li and Zhang
[
29
]
later treated the case with slip boundary conditions. Regarding the transition threshold problem, Antonelli, Dolce, and Marcati
[
1
]
proved linear stability and enhanced dissipation for two-dimensional Couette flow on
{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}
at high Reynolds number; they recovered the
t^{1/2}
growth of the
L^{2}
norm previously observed in the physical literature
[
21
]
. Zeng, Zi, and the third author
[
47
]
obtained analogous results on
{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{T}}
. Most recently, Huang, Li, and Xu
[
25
]
addressed the nonlinear problem and showed that
\beta\leq 11/3
when the perturbation space
X
is taken to be a Sobolev space.

The goal of this paper is to investigate the nonlinear stability of the Couette flow for the two-dimensional compressible Navier–Stokes equations and seek the optimal threshold exponent
\beta
.

1.2
Main result

Due to the different physical properties of the velocity field
\bm{v}
, we decompose it into two distinct components: the compressible part and the incompressible part. Specifically, we define

\displaystyle d\triangleq\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v},\quad\omega\triangleq\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}=\nabla^{\perp}_{\bm{x}}\cdot\bm{v}=-\partial_{y}v^{1}+\partial_{x}v^{2}.

(1.8)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\bm{v}=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{x}}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}+\nabla^{\perp}_{\bm{x}}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}=\nabla_{\bm{x}}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}d+\nabla^{\perp}_{\bm{x}}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}\omega.

(1.9)

In view of the system (
1
), we find that
(n,d,\omega)
satisfies the following system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}n+y\partial_{x}n+d=F_{1},\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}d+y\partial_{x}d+2\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}d+2\partial_{x}^{2}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}\omega+\Delta_{\bm{x}}n-\nu\Delta_{\bm{x}}d=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}{\bm{F}}_{2},\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}\omega+y\partial_{x}\omega-d-\mu\Delta_{\bm{x}}\omega=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}{\bm{F}}_{2},\\[4.30554pt]
(n,d,\omega)|_{t=0}=(n_{in},d_{in},\omega_{in})(\bm{x})=(n_{in},\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}_{in},\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v}_{in})(\bm{x}),\end{array}\right.

where
\nu\triangleq 2\mu+\mu^{\prime}
.

For any
s\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}
, we say that
q(x,y)\in{\mathcal{H}}^{s}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
whenever

\displaystyle\|q\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}^{2}\triangleq\sum_{|\alpha|\leq s}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}}|(\partial_{x},\partial_{y}+t\partial_{x})^{\alpha}q|^{2}\,dxdy<+\infty.

For any function
q
, we also define

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{0}q\triangleq\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}q(x,y)\,dx,\quad{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}q\triangleq q-{\mathbb{P}}_{0}q.

Now we are in a position to state our main result.

Theorem 1.1
.

There exists a small
\epsilon
independent of
\mu,\nu
, such that if
0<\nu,\mu\leq 1
,
\nu
has the same order of
\mu
, and the initial data
(n_{in},d_{in},w_{in})
satisfies

\displaystyle\|(n_{in},\bm{v}_{in})\|_{H^{4}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})}\leq\epsilon\mu,

(1.14)

then the system (
2.1
) admits a unique global solution
(n,d,\omega)
, such that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|({\mathbb{P}}_{0}n,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\Lambda^{-1}_{\bm{x}}d,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\omega)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}^{3}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}))}+\mu^{\frac{1}{6}}\|({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n,{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\Lambda^{-1}_{\bm{x}}d,{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\omega)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}^{3}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}))}\leq C\|(n_{in},\bm{v}_{in})\|_{H^{4}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})},

where
\Lambda^{s}_{\bm{x}}\triangleq(-\Delta_{\bm{x}})^{\frac{s}{2}}
and
C
is a constant independent of
t
and
\mu,\nu
.

Remark 1.2
.

Based on Theorem
1.1
, we can derive the decay rate for the zero mode of the solution as

\displaystyle\|({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y}n,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}d,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y}\omega)\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C(1+\mu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(1.15)

For non-zero mode of the solution, we can establish the following enhanced dissipation and inviscid damping estimates

\displaystyle\|({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n,{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\Lambda^{-1}_{\bm{x}}d)\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})}\leq C(1+t)^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\epsilon_{1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}t},

(1.16)

\displaystyle(1+t)\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\partial_{x}\Delta_{\bm{x}}^{-1}\omega\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})}+\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\partial_{y}\Delta_{\bm{x}}^{-1}\omega\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})}\leq C(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\epsilon_{1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}t}.

(1.17)

The decay estimate (
1.15
) can be derived by employing the time-weighted energy estimates.
The decay estimates (
1.16
)-(
1.17
) are immediate consequences of applying the weights
m_{1}
and
\varphi
in the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
Their proofs are therefore omitted.

Remark 1.3
.

Let us provide some discussions on the stability threshold of the initial data via a formal asymptotic analysis.

First, the linear stability analysis of Antonelli–Dolce–Marcati
[
1
]
shows that the linearized system associated with (
1.9
) loses a factor
\mu^{1/6}
; more precisely,

\displaystyle\|({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n,{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\bm{v})\|_{L^{2}}\leq\mu^{-1/6}\|(n_{in},\bm{v}_{in})\|_{L^{2}}.

Returning to the nonlinear system,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n=-{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}v^{2}\partial_{y}{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n+\cdots,

we obtain, schematically,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n\sim t\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}v^{2}\partial_{y}{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n+\cdots.

Enhanced dissipation dictates the time scale
t\sim\mu^{-\frac{1}{3}}
, while
\partial_{y}=(\partial_{y}-t\partial_{x})+t\partial_{x}
introduces an additional loss of
\mu^{-\frac{1}{3}}
. Collecting these losses, closing the energy estimates for the nonlinear problem requires at least
\|(n_{in},\bm{v}_{in})\|_{X}\sim\mu^{\alpha}
with
\alpha\geq 5/6
.

For compressible flows, however,
\alpha=5/6
is still insufficient because of the stronger lift-up effect. This marks another key difference from the incompressible setting, where—even in three dimensions—the lift-up mechanism appears only in the equation for
v^{1}
. Exploiting the divergence-free condition,
[
45
]
therefore starts from the subsystem
({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}v^{2},{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}v^{3})
and recovers
{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}v^{1}
a posteriori; in effect, they estimate
\|({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}v^{2},{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}v^{3})\|_{H^{2}}
rather than
\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\bm{v}\|_{H^{2}}
. Incompressible flows can thus circumvent the lift-up term for non-zero modes.
Compressible flows lack this structure. To tame the lift-up growth, the works
[
1
,
5
,
25
,
31
,
47
]
introduce the Fourier multiplier
\varphi
defined in (
3.1
), which satisfies
1\lesssim\varphi\lesssim\mu^{-2/3}
. Using this weight, we control
\|\varphi^{-1/4}\hat{n}\|_{H^{2}}
. Formally,

\displaystyle({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}[\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}]{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n)\sim t\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}v^{2}\partial_{y}({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}[\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}]{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n)+t[{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}[\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}],{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}v^{2}\partial_{y}]{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}n+\cdots,

(1.18)

where
\hat{n}
stands for the Fourier transform of
n
. The commutator sacrifices the weight
\varphi^{1/4}
, resulting in an additional loss of
\mu^{1/6}
. A similar loss already appears in
[
5
]
, where
\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\bm{v}\|_{H^{N}}
is estimated with the aid of the multiplier
\varphi
to counteract the lift-up effect. Guided by these considerations, the stability threshold of the initial data (
1.14
) seems optimal.

Throughout this paper, all constants
C
may be different in different lines, but universal. A constant with subscript(s) illustrates the dependence of the constant, for example,
C_{a}
is a constant which depends on
a
. For
a\lesssim b
, we mean that there exists a universal constant
C
, such that
a\leq Cb
. While
a\gtrsim b
, we mean that there exists a universal constant
C
, such that
a\geq Cb
. And
a\sim b
means that
a\lesssim b\lesssim a
.

2
Reformulation of the system and Main strategy

2.1
New formulation

One of the main differences between compressible and incompressible flows is that the lift-up effect already appears in two-dimensional compressible flows, whereas in the incompressible setting it is genuinely three-dimensional. This mechanism induces linear growth up to the time scale
t\leq\frac{1}{\mu}
. To mitigate the lift-up effect, Antonelli, Dolce, and Marcati
[
1
]
introduced a good unknown

\displaystyle w\triangleq\omega+n-\mu d,

(2.1)

which satisfies

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}n+y\partial_{x}n+d=F_{1},\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}d+y\partial_{x}d+2\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}d+2\partial_{x}^{2}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}(w-n+\mu d)+\Delta_{\bm{x}}n-\nu\Delta_{\bm{x}}d=F_{2},\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}w+y\partial_{x}w-\mu\Delta_{\bm{x}}w+\mu(\mu+\mu^{\prime})\Delta_{\bm{x}}d-2\mu\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}d-2\mu\partial_{x}^{2}\Delta^{-1}_{\bm{x}}(w-n+\mu d)=F_{3},\\[4.30554pt]
(n,d,w)|_{t=0}=(n_{in},d_{in},w_{in})(\bm{x})\triangleq(n_{in},d_{in},\omega_{in}+n_{in}-\mu d_{in})(\bm{x}),\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle F_{1}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\bm{v}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}n-nd,

\displaystyle F_{2}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\bm{v}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}d-F_{21},

\displaystyle F_{3}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\bm{v}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w-d(w+\mu d)-\nabla^{\perp}_{\bm{x}}\cdot\big(\frac{n}{1+n}(\nu\nabla_{\bm{x}}d+\mu\nabla^{\perp}_{\bm{x}}(w-n+\mu d))\big)+\mu F_{21},

with

\displaystyle F_{21}\triangleq

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}v^{1})^{2}+(\partial_{y}v^{2})^{2}+2\partial_{y}v^{1}\partial_{x}v^{2}+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\big(\big(\frac{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(1+n)}{1+n}-1\big)\nabla_{\bm{x}}n\big)

\displaystyle+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\big(\frac{n}{1+n}(\nu\nabla_{\bm{x}}d+\mu\nabla^{\perp}_{\bm{x}}(w-n+\mu d))\big).

Remark 2.1
.

We now highlight the advantages of the new formulation.

•

The equation for
w
contains no linear term in
d
, thereby eliminating a potential source of unbounded growth. This structural property is essential to our analysis.

•

For streak solutions, the system governing
({\mathbb{P}}_{0}n,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}d,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}w)
is completely devoid of lift-up terms. Consequently, we obtain uniform-in-time bounds for
({\mathbb{P}}_{0}n,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}d,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}w)
which is controlled by the initial data. Using the identity
{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\omega=\partial_{y}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}v^{1}
, we further derive comparable estimates for
({\mathbb{P}}_{0}n,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}v^{2},{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y}v^{1})
. Although
{\mathbb{P}}_{0}v^{1}
itself may grow in
L^{2}
, this growth does not propagate to the energy norms we control; hence the a priori estimates close.

•

For the non-zero mode, the lift-up effect appearing in the equation for
{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}w
is strictly weaker than that in the equation for
{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\omega
. We prove that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}w
do not lose
\mu^{\frac{1}{6}}
suffered by
{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\omega
. In this sense,
w
behaves exactly like the vorticity in two-dimensional incompressible flow.

2.2
Coordinates transformation

To eliminate the transport terms, such as
y\partial_{x}n
,
y\partial_{x}d
and
y\partial_{x}w
, from system (
2.1
), we introduce the moving-frame coordinates

\displaystyle t\triangleq t,\quad x_{1}\triangleq x-ty,\quad y_{1}\triangleq y.

(2.6)

In this frame we set

\displaystyle(N,D,W,\bm{V},P)(t,x_{1},y_{1})\triangleq(n,d,w,\bm{v},\mathcal{P})(t,x_{1}+ty_{1},y_{1}).

More generally, for any function
q(t,x,y)
(which may be
n,d,w,\bm{v}
), we define
Q(t,x_{1},y_{1})\triangleq q(t,x_{1}+ty_{1},y_{1})
under which the differential operators transform as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}q\triangleq\nabla_{\bm{x}}q=(\partial_{x_{1}}Q,(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})Q),\quad\widetilde{\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits}q\triangleq\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}q=\partial_{x_{1}}Q^{1}+(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})Q^{2},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}q\triangleq\Delta_{\bm{x}}q=\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}Q+(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})^{2}Q.

And we denote

\displaystyle\nabla Q\triangleq(\partial_{x_{1}}Q,\partial_{y_{1}}Q),\quad\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits Q\triangleq\partial_{x_{1}}Q^{1}+\partial_{y_{1}}Q^{2},\quad\Delta Q\triangleq\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}Q+\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}Q.

The system (
2.1
) is then reduced to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}N+D=\widetilde{F}_{1},\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}D+2\partial_{x_{1}}(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\widetilde{\Delta}^{-1}D+2\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{-1}(W-N+\mu D)+\widetilde{\Delta}N-\nu\widetilde{\Delta}D=\widetilde{F}_{2},\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}W-\mu\widetilde{\Delta}W+\mu(\mu+\mu^{\prime})\widetilde{\Delta}D-2\mu\partial_{x_{1}}(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\widetilde{\Delta}^{-1}D-2\mu\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{-1}(W-N+\mu D)=\widetilde{F}_{3},\\[4.30554pt]
(N,D,W)|_{t=0}=(n_{in},d_{in},w_{in})(x_{1},y_{1}),\end{array}\right.

where the source terms may be determined explicitly,

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{1}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\bm{V}\cdot\widetilde{\nabla}N-ND,

(2.11)

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{2}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\bm{V}\cdot\widetilde{\nabla}D-\widetilde{F}_{21},

(2.12)

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{3}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\bm{V}\cdot\widetilde{\nabla}W-D(W+\mu D)-\widetilde{\nabla}^{\perp}\cdot\left(g(N)(\nu\widetilde{\nabla}D+\mu\widetilde{\nabla}^{\perp}(W-N+\mu D))\right)+\mu\widetilde{F}_{21},

(2.13)

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{21}\triangleq

\displaystyle(\partial_{x_{1}}V^{1})^{2}+((\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})V^{2})^{2}+2(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})V^{1}\partial_{x_{1}}V^{2}+\widetilde{\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits}\left(f(N)\widetilde{\nabla}N\right)

(2.14)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits}\left(g(N)(\nu\widetilde{\nabla}D+\mu\widetilde{\nabla}^{\perp}(W-N+\mu D))\right),

and the nonlinear functions of
N
are defined by

\displaystyle f(N)\triangleq\frac{P^{\prime}(1+N)}{1+N}-1,\quad g(N)\triangleq\frac{N}{1+N}.

(2.15)

We introduce the definition of Sobolev space under the new coordinates. For any
s\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}
, we say that
Q(x_{1},y_{1})\in H^{s}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
whenever

\displaystyle\|Q\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\triangleq\sum_{|\alpha|\leq s}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}}|(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{y_{1}})^{\alpha}Q|^{2}\,dx_{1}dy_{1}<+\infty.

We note that for any
q(t,x,y)
and
Q(t,x_{1},y_{1})\triangleq q(t,x_{1}+ty_{1},y_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\|q\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}^{2}=\|Q\|_{H^{s}}^{2},\quad\forall~s\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}.

Then it is easy to see that Theorem
1.1
is a direct corollary of the following theorem.

Theorem 2.2
.

Under the assumptions in Theorem
1.1
, system (
2.2
) admits a unique global solution
(N,D,W)
such that, for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}(N,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}D)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}+\|W\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}\leq C\big(\|(n_{in},\Lambda^{-1}d_{in})\|_{H^{4}}+\|w_{in}\|_{H^{3}}\big),

and

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{6}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}(N,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}D)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}+\mu^{\frac{5}{6}}\|(\widetilde{\Lambda}N,D)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}\leq C\big(\|(n_{in},\Lambda^{-1}d_{in})\|_{H^{4}}+\|w_{in}\|_{H^{3}}\big),

where
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{s}\triangleq(-\widetilde{\Delta})^{\frac{s}{2}}
and

\Lambda^{s}\triangleq(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}
.

2.3
Main strategy

In this subsection, we outline the main ideas of this paper.

\bullet

Introducing a new formulation.

Guided by the linear analysis of Antonelli–Dolce–Marcati
[
1
]
, we recall that the linearised problem for (
2.1
) was treated as a
3\times 3
system leading to estimates for

(\varphi^{-\frac{3}{4}}(\widetilde{\Delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{N},\varphi^{-\frac{3}{4}}\hat{D},\varphi^{-\frac{3}{4}}\hat{w}),

where
\hat{q}
stands for the Fourier transform of
q
, and the weight
\varphi^{-\frac{3}{4}}
is introduced to control the lift-up effect. In this setting, we need to deal with the following nonlinear term

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}[\varphi^{-\frac{3}{4}}]({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}V\cdot\widetilde{\nabla}D)\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}V\cdot\widetilde{\nabla}({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}[\varphi^{-\frac{3}{4}}]{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}D)+[{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}[\varphi^{-\frac{3}{4}}],V\cdot\widetilde{\nabla}]{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}D+\cdots,

(2.16)

Here we notice that the commutator will bring a loss of
\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}
, preventing the desired transition threshold.

To overcome this, we return to the unknown
(n,\bm{v})
while retaining the favorable structure of (
2.1
). Define
{\mathcal{U}}=\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}D
; we then regard
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
as a
2\times 2
system and estimate
W
separately. Schematically, we first study

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}N+\widetilde{\Delta}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{U}}=\cdots,\\
\partial_{t}{\mathcal{U}}+2\partial_{x_{1}}(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\widetilde{\Delta}^{-1}{\mathcal{U}}+2\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{3}{2}}(W-N+\mu D)+\widetilde{\Delta}^{\frac{1}{2}}N-\nu\widetilde{\Delta}{\mathcal{U}}=\cdots,\end{array}\right.

Enhanced dissipation and inviscid damping are recovered by introducing Fourier multipliers
m_{1},m_{2}
, while the lift-up effect is tamed by the multiplier
\varphi^{\frac{1}{4}}
; see Subsection
3.1
. In this system, we use the weight
\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}
, which will bring better estimates, i.e., result in a loss of
\mu^{\frac{1}{6}}
in nonlinear estimates(see (
1.18
)). Moreover, the equation for
W
contains no lift-up term, and its energy estimate is carried out directly.

\bullet

Constructing suitable energy functionals.

Estimating
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
and
W
separately creates a new difficulty. On the one hand,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(N,{\mathcal{U}})\sim\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}W,

so
\|(N,{\mathcal{U}})\|_{H^{s}}
is controlled by
\|\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}W\|_{H^{s}}
. On the other hand, the equation for
\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}W
is

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{W})+\frac{\partial_{t}p}{2p}(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{W})=\cdots,\quad p=k^{2}+(\xi-kt)^{2},

where the second term arises from the commutator
[\partial_{t},p^{-\frac{1}{2}}]
. This term is not a lift-up contribution and cannot be absorbed by
\varphi
. This obstacle motivated
[
1
]
to keep the
3\times 3
system. We instead continue to work with
W
rather than
\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}W
; accordingly, we need
\|W\|_{H^{s}}
to control the energy. The price is that
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
now requires one higher derivative. Indeed, from

\displaystyle\partial_{t}W-\mu\widetilde{\Delta}W+\mu(\mu+\mu^{\prime})\widetilde{\Delta}D=\cdots,

the worst term is
\mu(\mu+\mu^{\prime})\widetilde{\Delta}D
, so
\|W\|_{H^{s}}
is control by
\mu\|(N,{\mathcal{U}})\|_{H^{s+1}}
. The prefactor
\mu
compensates the derivative loss, making the estimate straightforward. For this reason we introduce the additional high-order energy functional
{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com}(t)
for the compressible part
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
; details are given in Section
4
.

3
Introduction of Fourier multipliers

In this section, we introduce some key Fourier multipliers and collect definitions and auxiliary lemmas that will be used repeatedly in the energy estimates.

3.1
The Fourier multipliers

We recall that the symbols
p
and
\frac{2k\eta_{k}}{p}=-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}
correspond to the operators
-\widetilde{\Delta}
and
2\partial_{x_{1}}(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\widetilde{\Delta}^{-1}
, respectively. Since
2\frac{k\eta_{k}}{p}\hat{D}^{k}
is the bad term in equation
\eqref{eq_n-d-w-2}_{2}
, it is natural to study the toy model

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{D}^{k}=\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\hat{D}^{k}-\nu p\hat{D}^{k}.

(3.1)

For
t\leq\frac{\xi}{k}
, we have
\partial_{t}p=-2k(\xi-kt)\leq 0
; hence the first term on the right-hand side acts as a damping term for
\hat{D}^{k}
.
For
t>\frac{\xi}{k}
the derivative becomes positive,
\partial_{t}p>0
,
and the same term drives growth.
Near the critical time
t=\frac{\xi}{k}
the dissipation satisfies
\nu p\sim\nu k^{2}
;
thus the dissipation alone cannot uniformly control the growth in
\nu
.
Nevertheless, sufficiently far from the critical layer, dissipation prevails: for any fixed
\beta>2
, the inequality

\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\leq\frac{1}{4}\nu p,

holds whenever
|t-\frac{\xi}{k}|\geq\beta\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}
.

To exploit this dichotomy and allow the dissipative term
\nu p\hat{D}^{k}
to balance the growth caused by
\frac{2k\eta_{k}}{p}\hat{D}^{k}
, we follow
[
1
,
5
,
25
,
31
,
47
]
and introduce the Fourier multiplier
\varphi(t,k,\eta)
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\varphi=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}0,\quad t\not\in[\frac{\xi}{k},\frac{\xi}{k}+\beta\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}],\\[4.30554pt]
\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\varphi,\quad t\in[\frac{\xi}{k},\frac{\xi}{k}+\beta\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}],\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.\\
\varphi(0,k,\xi)=1,\end{array}\right.

where
\beta>2
is a constant determined later.
Specially, we have
\varphi(t,0,\xi)=1
.

In the energy estimates we also employ the following two Fourier multipliers to obtain enhanced dissipation and inviscid-damping bounds; they have been used extensively in
[
1
,
5
,
6
,
8
,
25
,
47
,
48
]
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}m_{1}(t,k,\xi)=\frac{2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}}{1+\nu^{\frac{2}{3}}(\frac{\xi}{k}-t)^{2}}m_{1}(t,k,\xi),\\[4.30554pt]
m_{1}(0,k,\xi)=1,\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}m_{2}(t,k,\xi)=A\frac{k^{2}}{p}m_{2}(t,k,\xi)=A\frac{1}{1+(\frac{\xi}{k}-t)^{2}}m_{2}(t,k,\xi),\\[4.30554pt]
m_{2}(0,k,\xi)=1,\end{array}\right.

where
A
is a large, but fixed constant, which will be determined later.
It is easy to check that, for any
t\geq 0
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}
, the quantities
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
can be expressed explicitly as

\displaystyle m_{1}(t,k,\xi)=\begin{cases}\mathop{\rm exp}\nolimits\left\{2\arctan\left(\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(t-\frac{\xi}{k}\right)\right)+2\arctan\left(\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{\xi}{k}\right)\right\},\quad\text{for}~~k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\},\\
1,\quad\text{for}~~k=0;\end{cases}

\displaystyle m_{2}(t,k,\xi)=\begin{cases}\mathop{\rm exp}\nolimits\left\{A\arctan\left(t-\frac{\xi}{k}\right)+A\arctan\left(\frac{\xi}{k}\right)\right\},\quad\text{for}~~k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\},\\
1,\quad\text{for}~~k=0.\end{cases}

These three Fourier multipliers introduced above enjoy the following crucial properties:

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
\varphi
,
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
be three Fourier multipliers defined in (
3.1
), (
3.1
) and (
3.1
).
Then the multipliers
\varphi
,
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
are bounded from above and below uniformly with respect to
0<\nu<1
and
(t,k,\xi)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\times{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathbb{R}}
. Specifically, they are bounded by

\displaystyle 1\leq\varphi\lesssim\nu^{-\frac{2}{3}},\quad 1\leq m_{1}\leq e^{2\pi},\quad 1\leq m_{2}\leq e^{A\pi}.

Moreover, for any
t\geq 0
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

\displaystyle 1\leq\varphi(t,k,\xi)\leq\beta^{2}\nu^{-\frac{2}{3}},\quad\frac{\varphi}{p}\leq\frac{1}{k^{2}}.

(3.9)

For any
\max\{2\beta^{-1}(\beta^{2}-1)^{-1},4\beta^{-1}\}<\delta_{\beta}\leq 1
with
\beta>2
, we also have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{\beta}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\nu p\right)+\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\geq\delta_{\beta}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}},\\[4.30554pt]
\delta_{\beta}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)+\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\geq\frac{\delta_{\beta}}{2}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}.\end{array}\right.

3.2
Some useful lemmas

In order to close the energy estimates for the solution, we need certain commutator estimates.
First, for any function
q
, we introduce the following operators:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}q\triangleq{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}[m_{1}^{-1}m_{2}^{-1}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}\hat{q}],\quad\mathcal{R}_{2}q\triangleq{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}[m_{1}^{-1}m_{2}^{-1}\hat{q}],

(3.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}q\triangleq{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\left[\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\hat{q}\right]=-\partial_{x_{1}}(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\widetilde{\Delta}^{-1}q,

(3.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{4}q\triangleq\partial_{y_{1}}|\partial_{y_{1}}|^{-1}q,\quad\mathcal{R}_{5}q\triangleq\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits}q.

(3.14)

We then recall the following two lemmas concerning commutator estimates.

Lemma 3.2
.

For any
\mathcal{R}_{i}(i=1,\cdots,5)
,
q\in L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
and
\widetilde{\nabla}\bm{V}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
, we have

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R}_{i},\bm{V}]\cdot\widetilde{\nabla}q\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{\nabla}\bm{V}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|q\|_{L^{2}}.

(3.15)

Proof.

First, we consider the case
\mathcal{R}_{3}
. By using coordinate transformation, we compute that

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R}_{3},\bm{V}]\cdot\widetilde{\nabla}q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}}|[\partial_{x_{1}}(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\widetilde{\Delta}^{-1},\bm{V}](\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})q|^{2}(t,x_{1},y_{1})\,dx_{1}dy_{1}

(3.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}}|[\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\Delta_{\bm{x}}^{-1},\bm{V}](\partial_{x},\partial_{y})q|^{2}(t,x-ty,y)\,dxdy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{(x,y)\in{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}}|(\partial_{x},\partial_{y})\bm{V}(t,x-ty,y)|^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}}|q(t,x-ty,y)|^{2}\,dxdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{(x_{1},y_{1})\in{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}}|(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\bm{V}(t,x_{1},y_{1})|^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}}|q(t,x_{1},y_{1})|^{2}\,dx_{1}dy_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\nabla}\bm{V}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

By the same argument, we can prove the cases for
\mathcal{R}_{4}
and
\mathcal{R}_{5}
.

We denote
m=m_{1}^{-1},m_{2}^{-1}
or
\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}
, and then we have

\displaystyle m{\mathcal{F}}[\bm{V}\cdot\nabla q]-\hat{\bm{V}}*(m{\mathcal{F}}[\nabla q])

(3.17)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(m(t,k,\xi)-m(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}))\hat{\bm{V}}(t,k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})\cdot(k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\hat{q}(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\,d\xi^{\prime}.

For
(k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
sufficiently large and far away from
(k,\xi)
, we have
|(k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})|\sim|(k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})|
.
Hence,

\displaystyle|(m(t,k,\xi)-m(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}))\hat{\bm{V}}(t,k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})\cdot(k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\hat{q}(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})|

(3.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|m\|_{L^{\infty}_{k,\xi}}|(k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})\hat{\bm{V}}(t,k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})||\hat{q}(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})|.

For
(k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
sufficiently small and far away from
(k,\xi)
or
|(k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})|\sim|(k,\xi)|
, we obtain

\displaystyle|(m(t,k,\xi)-m(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}))\hat{\bm{V}}(t,k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})\cdot(k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\hat{q}(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})|

(3.19)

\displaystyle=|\nabla m(t,\bar{k},\bar{\xi})\cdot(k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})\hat{\bm{V}}(t,k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})\cdot(k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\hat{q}(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(k,\xi)\nabla m\|_{L^{\infty}_{k,\xi}}|(k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})\hat{\bm{V}}(t,k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})||\hat{q}(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\lesssim|(k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})\hat{\bm{V}}(t,k-k^{\prime},\xi-\xi^{\prime})||\hat{q}(t,k^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})|.

Substituting (
3.18
) and (
3.19
) into (
3.17
), we find that

\displaystyle\|m{\mathcal{F}}[\bm{V}\cdot\nabla q]-\hat{\bm{V}}*(m{\mathcal{F}}[\nabla q])\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\|m\|_{L^{\infty}_{k,\xi}}+\|(k,\xi)\nabla m\|_{L^{\infty}_{k,\xi}}\right)\|{\mathcal{F}}[\nabla\bm{V}]*\hat{q}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla\bm{V}\cdot q\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla\bm{V}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|q\|_{L^{2}}.

By the same arguments used above, we conclude that estimate (
3.15
) holds for
i=1,2
.
∎

Lemma 3.3
.

For any
s\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}
,
g_{1}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
,
\nabla^{s-1}g_{1}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
,
g_{2}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
,
\widetilde{\nabla}^{s-1}g_{2}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
, and
\nabla\bm{V}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
,
\nabla^{s}\bm{V}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
,
\widetilde{\nabla}^{s}\bm{V}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}})
, we have

\displaystyle\|[\nabla^{s},\bm{V}]g_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla\bm{V}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{s-1}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{s}\bm{V}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}},

(3.20)

\displaystyle\|[\widetilde{\nabla}^{s},\bm{V}]g_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{\nabla}\bm{V}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\widetilde{\nabla}^{s-1}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\widetilde{\nabla}^{s}\bm{V}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

(3.21)

Proof.

The first commutator estimate (
3.20
) is standard; its proof is omitted here and the reader may consult
[
34
]
for details.
Following the same argument used to derive (
3.16
), the second commutator estimate (
3.21
) follows directly from (
3.20
).
∎

4
Construction of the energy functional

In this section we construct the energy functional and establish the
a priori
estimates for smooth local solutions of system (
2.2
). These estimates will allow us to extend the local solution
(N,D,W)
to a global one and play a fundamental role in the proof of Theorem
2.2
. We begin by taking the Fourier transform in both spatial variables
(x_{1},y_{1})
; thus, for any function
q(t,x_{1},y_{1})
, we set

\displaystyle\hat{q}^{k}(t,\xi)\triangleq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}}q(t,x_{1},y_{1})e^{-ikx_{1}-i\xi y_{1}}\,dx_{1}dy_{1},\quad\forall~k\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

Taking the Fourier transform of the system (
2.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\hat{N}^{k}+\hat{D}^{k}=\hat{\mathscr{F}}_{1}^{k},\quad t\in{\mathbb{R}}^{+},~~\xi\in{\mathbb{R}},~~k\in{\mathbb{Z}},\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{D}^{k}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\hat{D}^{k}+2\frac{k^{2}}{p}(\hat{W}^{k}-\hat{N}^{k}+\mu\hat{D}^{k})-p\hat{N}^{k}+\nu p\hat{D}^{k}=\hat{\mathscr{F}}_{2}^{k},\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{W}^{k}+\mu p\hat{W}^{k}-\mu(\mu+\mu^{\prime})p\hat{D}^{k}+\mu\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\hat{D}^{k}-2\mu\frac{k^{2}}{p}(\hat{W}^{k}-\hat{N}^{k}+\mu\hat{D}^{k})=\hat{\mathscr{F}}_{3}^{k},\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle(\hat{N}^{k},\hat{D}^{k},\hat{W}^{k})|_{t=0}=(\hat{n}_{in}^{k},\hat{d}_{in}^{k},\hat{w}_{in}^{k})(\xi),\end{array}\right.

where
p
is defined by

\displaystyle p\triangleq k^{2}+(\xi-kt)^{2},\quad\partial_{t}p=-2k(\xi-kt).

(4.5)

In the following analysis, we concentrate on system (
4
) and derive the
a priori
estimates. When constructing the energy estimates, we will apply the operators
\nabla
and
\widetilde{\nabla}
to the system (
4
) and refer to
\nabla
as good derivative and to
\widetilde{\nabla}
as bad derivative.

Therefore, we introduce the notations for the lower-order derivative of the compressible part
(N,\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}D)
: for any
0\leq s\leq 3
,
0\leq j\leq s
,

\displaystyle\hat{N}_{j,s-j}^{k}\triangleq

\displaystyle m_{1}^{-1}m_{2}^{-1}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}<k,\xi>^{s-j}p^{\frac{j}{2}}\hat{N}^{k},

(4.6)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{j,s-j}^{k}\triangleq

\displaystyle m_{1}^{-1}m_{2}^{-1}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}<k,\xi>^{s-j}p^{\frac{j-1}{2}}\hat{D}^{k},

(4.7)

where we use the notation
{\mathcal{U}}
represents the compressible part of the velocity, namely
{\mathcal{U}}=\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}D=\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits}\bm{V}
in (
4.7
).

For the incompressible part
W
, we define: for any
0\leq s\leq 3
,
0\leq j\leq s
,

\displaystyle\hat{W}_{j,s-j}^{k}\triangleq

\displaystyle m_{1}^{-1}m_{2}^{-1}<k,\xi>^{s-j}p^{\frac{j}{2}}\hat{W}^{k}.

(4.8)

To close the energy, we need the higher-order derivative of the compressible part
(N,\widetilde{\Delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}D)
, namely for any
0\leq s\leq 3
,
0\leq j\leq s
,

\displaystyle\hat{N}_{j+1,s-j}^{k}\triangleq m_{1}^{-1}m_{2}^{-1}<k,\xi>^{s-j}p^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\hat{N}^{k},

(4.9)

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{j,s-j}^{k}\triangleq m_{1}^{-1}m_{2}^{-1}<k,\xi>^{s-j}p^{\frac{j}{2}}\hat{D}^{k}.

(4.10)

Remark 4.1
.

We see that for any function
q
, the notation
q_{j,l}
represents the
j
-th order “bad derivative” and the
l
-th order “good derivative” of
q
.

Remark 4.2
.

For the zero mode of the solution, it is not difficult to check that

\displaystyle(\hat{N}_{j,s-j}^{0},\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{j,s-j}^{0},\hat{W}_{j,s-j}^{0},\hat{N}_{j+1,s-j}^{0},\hat{D}_{j,s-j}^{0})=(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{0},\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{0},\hat{W}_{0,s}^{0},\hat{N}_{1,s}^{0},\hat{D}_{0,s}^{0}),\quad\forall~0\leq j\leq s.

(4.11)

Now we are in a position to introduce the energy functional, which is decomposed into compressible and incompressible parts. For the compressible part, for any
j=0,\cdots,3
, we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com,l}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=j}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left(|\hat{N}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}+|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}\right)\,d\xi,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{com,l}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\mu\sum_{s=j}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}p|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi+(3-j)\mu\sum_{s=j+1}^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|\hat{N}_{j,s-j}^{0}|^{2}\,d\xi

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=j}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left(\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}+A\frac{k^{2}}{p}\right)\left(|\hat{N}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}+|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}\right)\,d\xi.

For the incompressible part, for any
j=0,\cdots,3
, we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{in}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=j}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|\hat{W}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{in}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\mu\sum_{s=j}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}p|\hat{W}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi+\sum_{s=j}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left(\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}+A\frac{k^{2}}{p}\right)|\hat{W}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi.

Moreover, we also need the high-order energy functional for compressible part: for
j=0,\cdots,3
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=j}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left(|\hat{N}_{j+1,s-j}^{k}|^{2}+|\hat{D}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}\right)\,d\xi,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{com}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\mu\sum_{s=j}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}p|\hat{D}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi+(3-j)\mu\sum_{s=j+1}^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|\hat{N}_{j+1,s-j}^{0}|^{2}\,d\xi

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=j}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left(\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}+A\frac{k^{2}}{p}\right)\left(|\hat{N}_{j+1,s-j}^{k}|^{2}+|\hat{D}_{j,s-j}^{k}|^{2}\right)\,d\xi.

Here
A
is a large yet fixed constant determined later.

Then it is easy to find that Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
2.2
are a direct corollary of the following energy estimates.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume that
(N,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}D)\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{4}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}))
and
W\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{3}({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}))
are the solution for system (
2.2
) such that, for any
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}{\mathcal{E}}(\tau)+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}(\tau)\,d\tau\leq\epsilon_{0}^{2}\mu^{2},

(4.12)

where
{\mathcal{E}}(t)
and
{\mathscr{D}}(t)
, for some positive constants
c_{j}(j=0,\cdots,3)
, are defined by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{3}c_{j}\mu^{\frac{2j}{3}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com,l}(t)+\tilde{\delta}_{1}{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{in}(t)+\tilde{\delta}_{2}\mu^{\frac{5}{3}}{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com}(t)\right),

(4.13)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{3}c_{j}\mu^{\frac{2j}{3}}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{com,l}(t)+\tilde{\delta}_{1}{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{in}(t)+\tilde{\delta}_{2}\mu^{\frac{5}{3}}{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{com}(t)\right).

(4.14)

Then for any
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}{\mathcal{E}}(\tau)+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}(0)+\epsilon_{0}^{3}\mu^{2}.

(4.15)

At the end of this section, using the definitions (
4.6
)–(
4.10
) and the
a priori
assumptions (
4.12
), we derive several estimates for
(n,\bm{v})
that will be used repeatedly in the energy estimates for the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Under the assumptions in Proposition
4.1
, there holds that for any
j=0,\cdots,3
,

\bullet

(N,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}D)
satisfies

\displaystyle~\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}(N,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}D)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{1}{3}}{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com,l}(t),

\displaystyle~\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}D\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}+\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}(N,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}D)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{com,l}(\tau)\,d\tau,

\displaystyle~\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}(N,|\partial_{y_{1}}|^{-1}D)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t),

\displaystyle~\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}}^{2}+\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}D\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(\tau)\,d\tau.

\bullet

W
satisfies

\displaystyle~\|\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}W\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{in}(t),

\displaystyle~\mu\|\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j+1}W\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}+\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}W\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{in}(\tau)\,d\tau.

\bullet

(\widetilde{\Lambda}N,D)
satisfies

\displaystyle~\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}(\widetilde{\Lambda}N,D)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{5}{3}}{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com}(t),

\displaystyle~\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j+1}D\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}+\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}(\widetilde{\nabla}N,D)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{5}{3}}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{com}(\tau)\,d\tau,

\displaystyle~\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}(\partial_{y_{1}}N,D)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{5}{3}}{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com}(t),

\displaystyle~\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}}^{2}+\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}D\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{5}{3}}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com}(\tau)\,d\tau.

\bullet

\bm{V}
satisfies

\displaystyle~\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}\bm{V}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com,l}+{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{in}\right)(t),

\displaystyle~\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j+1}\bm{V}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}+\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}\bm{V}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau,

\displaystyle~\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j+1}\bm{V}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{5}{3}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com}+{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{in}\right)(t),

\displaystyle~\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j+2}\bm{V}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}+\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j+1}\bm{V}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{5}{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{com}+{\mathscr{D}}_{j,3-j}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

\bullet

({\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{1},{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2})
satisfies

\displaystyle~\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}V^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}}^{2}+\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\left({\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(t),

\displaystyle~\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}V^{1}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}}^{2}+\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}V^{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau,

\displaystyle~\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}V^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}+\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{5}{3}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com}+{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(t),

\displaystyle~\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}V^{1}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}+\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}V^{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{5}{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

Proof.

Recalling the definitions in (
4.6
)–(
4.8
) and invoking (
3.9
) together with the
a priori
assumptions (
4.12
), we immediately obtain the desired estimates for
(N,\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}D)
and
W
.

Thanks to
|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j-1}q|\leq|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}q|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}\bm{V}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j-1}D\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}+\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j-1}(W-N+\mu D)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j-1}D\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}+\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}(W,N)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3-j}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{2j}{3}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{com,l}(t)+{\mathcal{E}}_{j,3-j}^{in}(t)\right),

which implies the estimates of
\bm{V}
.

Thanks to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}D={\mathbb{P}}_{0}|\partial_{y_{1}}|^{-1}\partial_{y_{1}}V^{2},\quad{\mathbb{P}}_{0}W={\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}V^{1},

we find that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}V^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}(W-N+\mu D)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}}^{2}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)+{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t).

For
{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}
, we notice that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}|\partial_{y_{1}}|^{-1}D\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}}^{2}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t).

The remaining estimates for
({\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{1},{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2})
follow from the same argument; we leave the details to the reader. ∎

5
Energy estimate of compressible part

In this section, we establish the
a priori
energy estimates for the lower-order derivatives of the compressible part (the density and the divergence) of the solution. The argument follows a standard bootstrap procedure. First, we assume

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq N+1\leq 2,\quad\textrm{for all}\quad t\in[0,\infty).

(5.1)

It is not difficult to deduce that for any positive integer
s
,

\displaystyle|(f(N),g(N))|\lesssim|N|,\quad|(f^{(s)}(N),g^{(s)}(N))|\lesssim 1.

(5.2)

The main result of this section is stated as follows.

Proposition 5.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.2
and (
5.1
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(0)+\frac{1}{A}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}(\tau)\,d\tau+\mu^{-1}\left(|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|{\mathcal{E}}_{1,2}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right.

(5.3)

\displaystyle\left.+|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+\mu^{\frac{2}{3}}{\mathscr{D}}_{1,2}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau,

where
A
is a large but fixed constant, which will be determined later.

Before proceeding to the proof of Proposition
5.1
, we first state two auxiliary propositions — these are the key ingredients in establishing Proposition
5.1
.
It is well-known that the compressible Navier–Stokes equations satisfy an energy equality in
L^{2}
. Motivated by this observation and because an
L^{2}
estimate for
{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{1}
is unavailable, we derive an independent
L^{2}
estimate for
({\mathbb{P}}_{0}N,{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2})
with the aim of eliminating the undesired terms containing
{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{1}
.
To this end, we introduce the following definitions:

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left(|\hat{N}_{0,0}^{0}|^{2}+|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,0}^{0}|^{2}\right)\,d\xi,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[|\hat{N}_{0,0}^{k}|^{2}+|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,0}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,0}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,0}^{k}\right)\right]\,d\xi

(5.5)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=1}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}+|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right]\,d\xi

\displaystyle-2\delta_{1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\sum_{s=0}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}p^{-\frac{1}{2}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\,d\xi-2\delta_{1}\mu\sum_{s=1}^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[|\xi|^{-1}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{0}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{0}\right)\right]\,d\xi,

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{1}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\nu\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|\xi|^{2}|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,0}^{0}|^{2}\,d\xi,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{2}(t)\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)+\delta_{1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(1+2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\right]|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi

(5.7)

\displaystyle+\delta_{1}\mu\sum_{s=1}^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{0}|^{2}\,d\xi+\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)+\nu p\right]|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,0}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=1}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)+\nu p\right]|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi.

Here
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}(t)
and
\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{1}(t)
represent
L^{2}
-norm for the zero-mode of density and the compressible part of the velocity.
And
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}(t)
and
\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{2}(t)
involve the remaining terms in
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)
and
{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)
, respectively. More precisely, we find, by some direct computations, that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)\sim\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}(t)+\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}(t),\quad{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)\sim\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{1}(t)+\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{2}(t).

Thus, Proposition
5.1
is a direct consequence of the following Propositions
5.2
-
5.3
.

Firstly, we derive the
L^{2}
estimate for the zero-mode of the density and the compressible part of the velocity.

Proposition 5.2
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.2
and (
5.1
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{1}(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle~\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}(0)+\mu^{-1}\left(|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

(5.8)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+\mu^{\frac{2}{3}}{\mathscr{D}}_{1,2}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

In order to complete the proof of Proposition
5.1
, we need to estimate
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}(t)
and
\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{2}(t)
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.2
and (
5.1
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{2}(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle~\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}(0)+\frac{1}{A}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}(\tau)\,d\tau+\mu^{-1}\left(|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|{\mathcal{E}}_{1,2}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right.

(5.9)

\displaystyle\left.+|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+\mu^{\frac{2}{3}}{\mathscr{D}}_{1,2}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau,

where
A
is a large but fixed constant, which will be determined later.

5.1
Proof of Proposition
5.2

In this subsection, we intend to establish the
L^{2}
energy estimate for
{\mathbb{P}}_{0}(N_{0,0},{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,0})
.
To proceed, we exploit the structural properties of the governing equation.
First, notice that
{\mathbb{P}}_{0}{\mathcal{U}}_{0,0}=|\partial_{y_{1}}|^{-1}\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}
.
Consequently, recalling that
(\rho,v^{2})
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\rho+y\partial_{x}\rho+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}(\rho\bm{v})=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\rho(\partial_{t}v^{2}+y\partial_{x}v^{2}+\bm{v}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}v^{2})+\partial_{y}\mathcal{P}(\rho)=\mu\Delta_{\bm{x}}v^{2}+(\mu+\mu^{\prime})\partial_{y}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\bm{x}}\bm{v},\end{array}\right.

Recalling the setting (
2.6
) and setting

\displaystyle(\varrho,P)(t,x_{1},y_{1})\triangleq(\rho,\mathcal{P})(t,x_{1}+ty_{1},y_{1}),

then the system (
5.1
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\varrho+\widetilde{\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits}(\varrho\bm{V})=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\varrho(\partial_{t}V^{2}+\bm{V}\cdot\widetilde{\nabla}V^{2})+(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})P(\varrho)=\mu\widetilde{\Delta}V^{2}+(\mu+\mu^{\prime})(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\widetilde{\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits}\bm{V}.\end{array}\right.

We apply the operator
{\mathbb{P}}_{0}
to system (
5.1
) and find that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho+\partial_{y_{1}}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2})=G_{1},\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho(\partial_{t}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}+{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2})+\partial_{y_{1}}P({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)-\nu\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}=G_{2},\end{array}\right.

where
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
are defined by

\displaystyle G_{1}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}V^{2}),

\displaystyle G_{2}\triangleq

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\bm{V}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\nabla}V^{2})-{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho\left({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(\varrho)}{\varrho}\right)-\frac{P^{\prime}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}\right)

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(\varrho)}{\varrho}\right)\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\varrho\right)+\nu{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho\left({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}\right)-\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}\right)\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}\right){\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}(\mu\widetilde{\Delta}V^{2}+(\mu+\mu^{\prime})(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\widetilde{\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits}\bm{V})\right).

We employ Sobolev inequality and the a
priori
assumption (
4.12
) to obtain

\displaystyle\varrho\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho\sim 1.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

By standard
L^{2}
energy estimate, we can deduce from (
5.1
) that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left({\mathcal{G}}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}|^{2}\right)\,dy_{1}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}|^{2}\,dy_{1}

(5.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}G_{1}\int^{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}\frac{P(s)-P(1)}{s^{2}}\,dsdy_{1}+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}G_{1}\frac{P({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)-P(1)}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}\,dy_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}G_{1}|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}|^{2}\,dy_{1}+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}G_{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\,dy_{1}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{4}H_{i},

where
{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)
is defined by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)\triangleq{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho\int^{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}\frac{P(s)-P(1)}{s^{2}}\,ds.

(5.17)

Clearly, it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\varrho={\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N,\quad\partial_{y_{1}}\varrho=\partial_{y_{1}}N,

which, together with integration by parts, Sobolev inequality and Lemma
4.1
, gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|H_{1}|(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}V^{2})\frac{P({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)-P(1)}{({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)^{2}}\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho\right|\,dy_{1}d\tau

(5.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}V^{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}N\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}N\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{2}{3}}{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

By Sobolev inequality and Lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|H_{2}|(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}V^{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}N\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}

(5.19)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{2}{3}}|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

Following a similar line of reasoning as in the proof above, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|H_{3}|(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}V^{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}^{2}

(5.20)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{2}{3}}{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

Recalling the definition of
G_{2}
,
H_{4}
can be divided into the following five terms:

\displaystyle H_{4}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\bm{V}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\nabla}V^{2}){\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\,dy_{1}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho\left({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(\varrho)}{\varrho}\right)-\frac{P^{\prime}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}\right){\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\,dy_{1}

(5.21)

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(\varrho)}{\varrho}\right)\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\varrho\right){\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\,dy_{1}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho\left({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\frac{1}{\varrho}-\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}\right)\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\,dy_{1}

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}\right){\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}(\mu\widetilde{\Delta}V^{2}+(\mu+\mu^{\prime})(\partial_{y_{1}}-t\partial_{x_{1}})\widetilde{\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits}\bm{V})\right){\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\,dy_{1}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{5}H_{4i}.

One can easily verify that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|H_{41}|(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\bm{V}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\nabla}V^{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}

(5.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}\left(|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

Since
\varrho={\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho+{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N
, for any smooth function
h(\rho)
, we have

\displaystyle h(\varrho)=h({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)+h^{\prime}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho){\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N+O(({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N)^{2}).

This yields that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|H_{42}|(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}

(5.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{5}{6}}\left(|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(\tau)\,d\tau.

A short calculation proves that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|H_{43}|(\tau)\,d\tau=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(\varrho)}{\varrho}-1\right)\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\varrho\right){\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\,dy_{1}d\tau

(5.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{2}{3}}\left(|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(\tau)\,d\tau.

Since

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}\right)-\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}-\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}\right)\,dx=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho-\varrho}{\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\varrho{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}\right)\right)\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho}={\mathbb{P}}_{0}\left({\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}\right)\right)\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho},

and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}\right)={\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\left(\frac{1}{N+1}-1\right)={\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\frac{N}{N+1},

(5.25)

we then have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|H_{44}|(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}

(5.26)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)+{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)\right)\int_{0}^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(\tau)\,d\tau.

While it is easy to observe, by applying (
5.25
) and Lemma
4.1
, that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|H_{45}|(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}N\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\not=}\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}\bm{V}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}

(5.27)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left(|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+\mu^{\frac{2}{3}}{\mathscr{D}}_{1,2}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

Substituting (
5.22
)-(
5.24
), (
5.26
) and (
5.27
) into (
5.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|H_{4}|(\tau)\,d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}\left(|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+\mu^{\frac{2}{3}}{\mathscr{D}}_{1,2}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

(5.28)

Plugging (
5.18
)-(
5.20
) and (
5.28
) into (
5.16
), and applying the
a priori
assumption (
4.12
), we find that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left({\mathcal{G}}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho)+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\varrho|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}|^{2}\right)\,dy_{1}+\nu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|\partial_{y_{1}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}V^{2}|^{2}\,dy_{1}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left({\mathcal{G}}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\rho_{in})+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}\rho_{in}|{\mathbb{P}}_{0}v_{in}^{2}|^{2}\right)\,dy+\mu^{-1}\left(|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{com,l}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,3}^{in}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{com,l}+\mu^{\frac{2}{3}}{\mathscr{D}}_{1,2}^{com,l}+{\mathscr{D}}_{0,3}^{in}\right)(\tau)\,d\tau.

This, along with (
5.4
), (
5.6
) and (
5.17
), implies that (
5.8
) holds directly.
∎

5.2
Proof of Proposition
5.3

Recalling the definition of (
4.6
) and (
4.7
) for
j=0
, it follows from the system (
4
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}=

\displaystyle-\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)\right]\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}-\frac{1}{4}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}-p^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}+\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{k},

(5.29)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}=

\displaystyle-\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)+\nu p\right]\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}+\left(p^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}

(5.30)

\displaystyle-2\mu\frac{k^{2}}{p}\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}-2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}\hat{W}_{0,s}^{k}+\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{k},

where
\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{k}
and
\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{k}
are defined by

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{k}\triangleq

\displaystyle m_{1}^{-1}m_{2}^{-1}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}<k,\xi>^{s}\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1}^{k},

(5.31)

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{k}\triangleq

\displaystyle m_{1}^{-1}m_{2}^{-1}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}<k,\xi>^{s}p^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2}^{k}.

(5.32)

Making use of equations (
5.29
) and (
5.30
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}|(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k},\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k})|^{2}+\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)\right]|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}

(5.33)

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)+\nu p\right]|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\left(|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}-|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}\right)+2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)-2\mu\frac{k^{2}}{p}|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}-2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}Re\left(\hat{W}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)

\displaystyle+Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{N}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)+Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right).

In order to cancel the first term presented on the right-hand side of (
5.33
), we find that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left[\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right]

(5.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\left(|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}-|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}\right)+2\frac{k^{2}\partial_{t}p}{p^{3}}|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}-\left[\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left(2\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+2\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}+\nu p\right)+2\mu\frac{k^{2}\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{5}{2}}}-2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{(\partial_{t}p)^{2}}{p^{\frac{5}{2}}}\right]Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)

\displaystyle-2\frac{k^{2}\partial_{t}p}{p^{3}}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{W}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)+\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left[Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)+Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{N}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right].

Multiplying equality (
5.34
) by
\frac{1}{4}
, adding the resulting equality to (
5.33
), then performing the summation and integration, we infer that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\{\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[|\hat{N}_{0,0}^{k}|^{2}+|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,0}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,0}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,0}^{k}\right)\right]\,d\xi\right.

(5.35)

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{s=1}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}+|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right]\,d\xi\right\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=0}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)\right]|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)+\nu p\right]|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,0}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=1}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}\right)+\nu p\right]|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\frac{k^{2}\partial_{t}p}{p^{3}}|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}-2\mu\frac{k^{2}}{p}|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}+\left[\frac{5}{2}\frac{k^{2}}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}-\frac{\mu}{2}\frac{k^{2}\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{5}{2}}}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{8}\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p}+\frac{1}{4}\nu p\right)\right]Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{2}\frac{k^{2}\partial_{t}p}{p^{3}}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{W}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)-2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}Re\left(\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{W}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right\}\,d\xi

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,0}^{k}\bar{\hat{N}}_{0,0}^{k}\right)+Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,0}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,0}^{k}\right)\right]\,d\xi

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=1}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{N}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)+Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right]\,d\xi

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{s=0}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{\partial_{t}p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left[Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)+Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{N}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right]\,d\xi.

Next, we aim to recover the dissipation of
\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}
as

\displaystyle-\frac{d}{dt}\left[p^{-\frac{1}{2}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right]+\left(1+2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}

(5.36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(2\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+2\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}+2\mu\frac{k^{2}}{p}+\nu p\right)Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)

\displaystyle+2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{2}}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{W}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)-\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left[Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)+Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{N}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right].

We note that the dissipation estimates of the density for zero and non-zero frequencies are different.
Multiplying equality (
5.36
) by
\delta_{1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}
, performing an integration with respect to
\xi
, then summing over
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}
and
s
from
0
to
3
, we obtain

\displaystyle-\delta_{1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{d}{dt}\sum_{s=0}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[p^{-\frac{1}{2}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right]\,d\xi+\delta_{1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\sum_{s=0}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left(1+2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}\,d\xi

(5.37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\sum_{s=0}^{3}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\{0\}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{|\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{0,s}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(2\frac{\partial_{t}m_{1}}{m_{1}}+2\frac{\partial_{t}m_{2}}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi}{\varphi}+2\mu\frac{k^{2}}{p}+\nu p\right)Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\frac{k^{2}}{p^{2}}\varphi^{-\frac{1}{4}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{W}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)-\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left[Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)+Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{k}\bar{\hat{N}}_{0,s}^{k}\right)\right]\right\}\,d\xi.

On the other hand, we multiply equality (
5.36
) by
\delta_{1}\mu
for
k=0
, perform an integration with respect to
\xi
, then sum over
s
from
1
to
3
to obtain

\displaystyle-\delta_{1}\mu\frac{d}{dt}\sum_{s=1}^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left[|\xi|^{-1}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{0}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{0}\right)\right]\,d\xi+\delta_{1}\mu\sum_{s=1}^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|\hat{N}_{0,s}^{0}|^{2}\,d\xi

(5.38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{1}\mu\sum_{s=1}^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{|\hat{D}_{0,s}^{0}|^{2}+\nu p^{\frac{1}{2}}Re\left(\hat{N}_{0,s}^{0}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{0}\right)-|\xi|^{-1}\left[Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{1,0,s}^{0}\bar{\hat{{\mathcal{U}}}}_{0,s}^{0}\right)+Re\left(\hat{{\mathscr{F}}}_{2,0,s}^{0}\bar{\hat{N}}_{0,s}^{0}\right)\right]\right\}\,d\xi.

Plugging (
5.35
), (
5.37
) and (
5.38
) together, we find that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}(t)+\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{2}(t)={\mathcal{L}}_{1}(t)+\sum_{i=1}^{5}I_{i}(t),

(5.39)

where
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}(t)
and
\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}_{2}(t)
are defined in (
5.5
) and (
5.7
), respectively.
And the linear term
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
is defined by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{1}(t)\triangleq
